Southwest Airlines Offers “Free” Same-Day Flight Changes, But There’s A Seat-Fee Trap

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Southwest Airlines offers eligible passengers free same-day flight changes, but there is a catch if you paid separately for your seat. Asking an airport agent to make the change can leave that seat purchase nonrefundable, while making the change through the app, website, or telephone receives different treatment. Apparently, how you change your flight now matters almost as much as which flight you choose.

View From The Wing highlights a peculiar provision in Southwest’s seat refund and exchange policy.

For passengers who separately purchased a seat on their original flight, Southwest explicitly states:

“Same-day change requested at the airport: Seat purchased on original flight is non-refundable.”

But complete that same-day change through Southwest.com, the Southwest app, or by telephone, and the rules provide a way to apply the original seat payment toward a replacement or receive a refund when buying a more expensive seat.

You could be standing at the gate with your phone in hand, looking at an earlier departure. Whether you tap the screen or ask the employee in front of you for help could affect what happens to money you already spent.

The policy concerns seats purchased separately from the airfare. Here is how Southwest describes the treatment when you voluntarily switch flights:

For a hypothetical example, suppose you paid $40 for a seat and make a confirmed same-day change through the app. If the replacement seat costs $30, Southwest applies the original payment but does not return the $10 difference.

If the replacement costs $50, you pay $50 upfront and generally receive a separate $40 refund. You should therefore check that the refund actually arrives rather than assuming the system simply charged you the difference.

Under Southwest’s same-day change policy, the confirmed-change benefit is available with Choice, Choice Preferred, and Choice Extra fares, along with Getaways by Southwest vacation bookings.

You need an available seat on a flight between the same origin and destination airports on the same travel date. The change must occur at least 10 minutes before your original scheduled departure.

Basic passengers must upgrade to an eligible fare first (and A-List or A-List Preferred status alone does not make a Basic ticket eligible for a free confirmed same-day change)

So when it comes to standby, there are two separate things to check: whether you can switch flights without an additional airfare charge, and what happens to the seat you purchased.

Do not confuse a confirmed change with same-day standby.

A confirmed change secures your place on the replacement flight immediately. Standby means waiting for space to become available, often much closer to boarding or even after boarding commences if you are waiting for no-shows. Southwest’s seat policy says the original seat purchase becomes nonrefundable when standby clears, regardless of how you joined the list.

Using the app therefore does not solve the seat-payment issue for standby. The eligibility rules differ too. A-List and A-List Preferred members can qualify for standby on Basic fares, even though those fares do not qualify for free confirmed same-day changes.

Of course, switching flights does not guarantee the same seat. Someone who bought extra legroom on one departure cannot reasonably expect an identical seat to materialize on a replacement flight. But why should the channel used to request the change determine the treatment of the original payment?

If Southwest can apply or refund that purchase when I use its app, why should asking an airport employee produce a less favorable rule? There may be a systems explanation, but that does little for a passenger who reasonably assumes an agent can provide the same options available online. On the other hand, I would bet that an agent who assigns you a superior seat in these sorts of situations is not going to charge for it…any frequent Southwest travelers want to weigh in?

This also puts employees in an awkward position. A passenger asks for help, receives it, then discovers that handling the transaction another way could have preserved the value of a seat purchase. For an airline that built so much goodwill around simplicity, this seems like an unnecessarily complicated way to handle a relatively ordinary request.

If you paid separately for a Southwest seat and want to make a confirmed same-day flight change, check the app or website before asking an airport agent to process it. Telephone changes also receive the more favorable treatment outlined in the policy.

Compare the replacement seat cost and keep track of any refund due. If you are using standby, remember that the app does not preserve the refundability of your original seat purchase once you clear.

A free flight change can still have consequences for the extras you purchased. Southwest passengers now have another reason to read the fine print before accepting what sounds like a straightforward benefit.

SpaceX now fundamentally an "infrastructure company," senior official says

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

“Grok analyzing the same video is 99.9 percent perfect.”

For a long time, SpaceX has been synonymous with the development of newer, larger, and ever-more-reusable rockets. But that began to change over the last decade with the introduction of Starlink, and the change has accelerated during the last 18 months as the company has pivoted to data and artificial intelligence.

At last week’s UP.Summit conference in Florida, the vice president of launch at SpaceX, Kiko Dontchev, offered some of the most revealing comments to date about the company’s changing focus, how it is embracing AI, and why the Falcon 9 rocket—which has dominated global launch for a decade and remains the world’s preeminent launch vehicle—is firmly on the road to retirement.

“In the last couple years, when I talk about SpaceX, yes, we are a rocket company, but fundamentally we are an infrastructure company at this point,” Dontchev said. “We’re building infrastructure on the ground and low Earth orbit, soon on the Moon, and obviously eventually Mars.”

Earlier this year, SpaceX acquired one of founder Elon Musk’s other companies, xAI. SpaceX engineers were already using xAI’s tools, Dontchev said, but in recent months, employees have really started to figure out how artificial intelligence can speed up their work.

“What’s happened at SpaceX is we’ve really gone through a huge acceleration of adoption,” Dontchev said. “Even just a year ago, we weren’t adopting it that quickly, but really starting this year, we started to adopt it extremely rapidly. And the acceleration we have seen in our ability to do engineering is unlike anything I’ve ever seen at the company in my 16 years.”

Tasks that would take SpaceX employees hours to days now take seconds to minutes, he said. And it’s not just the engineers. Dontchev said the work of identifying foreign object debris, or FOD, in aerospace parts has been largely automated using the AI assistant Grok. This debris can cause catastrophic damage to engines and other spacecraft components.

“We’ve automated being able to detect FOD,” he said. “We’ll have an inspector go in and borescope a tube, and we find that humans are about 94 percent perfect (at) finding a piece of it. Grok analyzing the same video is 99.9 percent perfect.”

SpaceX has also automated visual inspections of other hardware it produces, such as Starlink customer terminals, due to similar gains in accuracy. Dontchev said AI is now being used at every level of the company’s operations.

“We’ve truly started to integrate it to where every single person, including the technicians and the technician leaders, are starting to use it in their day-to-day, and it’s completely leveled the playing field and accelerated people’s growth,” he said.

Since SpaceX’s founding in 2002, a community of spaceflight enthusiasts gravitated to the company because of Musk’s vision for humanity becoming a multiplanetary species, beginning with the settlement of Mars. But concern has grown in this group that SpaceX is drifting away from that vision. Musk has recently made a hard pivot toward the deployment of orbital data centers, envisioning hundreds of thousands of solar-powered satellites providing computing services to users on Earth. Musk has largely stopped talking about sending humans to Mars, except in passing.

Dontchev insisted that human settlement and Mars remain part of the core of SpaceX’s aspirations.

“We have come to understand that this tool is critical to us accelerating our pace of innovation,” he said of artificial intelligence. “Without AI, I think we still would have gotten to the Moon. I think we still get to Mars with SI. We’re going to get there so much faster.”

Dontchev arrived at SpaceX just before the company flew its first Falcon 9 rocket in mid-2010. But because the company was never able to solve second-stage reuse, SpaceX needs to move on, he said.

“Falcon 9, I love that rocket to heart,” he said. “I’ve launched many of them, all of them, in fact. But it’s still not fully reusable. We still throw that second stage away every single flight. Starship—we’re going to catch both the first stage and the second stage.”

For 100 launches a year, it is possible to fly a partially reusable rocket, he said. But for a company that aspires to launch thousands of rockets a year, and eventually maybe even 10,000, the cost and time lost in manufacturing a new second stage for every launch is simply not sustainable.

It is also significant that Dontchev spoke less about Starship’s payload capacity and more about its productivity per launch. The first orbital Starship launch, in late September, carried larger Starlink V3 satellites. And in the aggregate, this single mission carried 10 times the bandwidth of the Starlink payload launched by a Falcon 9.

Flying Starship from Florida, he said, will further improve capacity compared to Texas launches. Launching from Florida offers more favorable trajectories to certain orbits, potentially allowing Starship to carry more satellites without expending propellant on dogleg maneuvers. Dontchev said a single Florida Starship launch will be able to carry up to 20 times the bandwidth of a single Falcon 9.

To that end, the pad infrastructure for Starship’s first launch tower at Kennedy Space Center’s Launch Complex 39A is nearly finished. The company is also developing two additional pads in Florida.

“Pad 39A is effectively complete,” he said. “We’re going to transport boosters and ships over barge here to the Cape. We’re hoping to actually do the first transports this year in December, and then maybe even fly by the end of the year.”

Starship is clearly being optimized to serve SpaceX’s launch needs. But what about commercial launch customers that have relied on Falcon 9 rockets over the last decade?

During his remarks, Dontchev did not address widespread concerns in the US commercial space industry and beyond that SpaceX has not moved to replace the Falcon 9’s lost capacity with Starship. For now, multiple sources have indicated, SpaceX is holding Starship launches in the coming years for its own needs, and potentially those of NASA and other government customers.

With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Is This Pete Hegseth’s Last Stand?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Secretary of Defense Pete Hegseth and his “patriotic cowboys” at the Pentagon used to warn America’s adversaries fuck around and find out. After Tuesday’s election, it looks like Hegseth is the one who is going to find out.

Sources on Capitol Hill and in military circles think Hegseth’s time at the Pentagon is coming to an end with Rep. Thomas Massie, a lame duck Kentucky Republican, planning to introduce articles of impeachment this week. But defense officials are cautioning that the embattled defense secretary has survived numerous missteps.

In response to calls for his impeachment and firing, Hegseth launched a coordinated PR campaign with a short video and photos across Pentagon social media channels of Hegseth with “STAND. FIGHT. DEFEND THE REPUBLIC.” tagline. Chief Pentagon Spokesman Sean Parnell says Hegseth won’t be deterred from executing President Trump’s agenda in the face of “sham witch hunts” and “phony investigations.” Parnell says Hegseth is committed to leading the Defense Department until the end of Trump’s term.

So far, Trump is supporting Hegseth, telling reporters the secretary is doing a good job, but reports before the election said the president asked advisers whether Hegseth should stay.

“It looks like his days are very seriously numbered,” James R. Webb, a Marine Iraq combat veteran who worked as a military legislative assistant for Sen. Rand Paul, tells me. “If they keep him, he’s probably going to be the first guy impeached by the new Congress.”

The threat of impeachment had been building before the election and could grow now that Democrats hold strong majorities in both chambers. In September, Massie introduced articles of impeachment that accuse Hegseth of violating the War Powers Resolution, ignoring laws meant to minimize civilian casualties, and carrying out extrajudicial killings in the boat-strike campaign against suspected drug traffickers, which left at least 221 dead.  Massie told CNN last week the only thing that would prevent him from introducing articles of impeachment when Congress reconvenes is Hegseth’s resignation.  Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

One rumored soft landing for Hegseth was Marsha Blackburn’s Senate seat, now vacant after her victory in the Tennessee governor race. But that was shot down when Jake Sherman of Punchbowl News reported Hegseth wasn’t being considered, which didn’t surprise Webb.

“He has basically burned every bridge possible with people on the Hill,” Webb says. “They don’t like him.”

With no apparent chance at Tennessee’s vacant Senate seat, Hegseth appears ready to fight, comparing himself to Trump in the almost-six-minute video Thursday.

“It’s all about the politics of personal destruction,” Hegseth says about Massie’s articles of impeachment. “Didn’t work against President Trump, and it won’t work with me.”

A former senior defense official who worked with Hegseth calls the video — which featured tough talk, culture war tropes, and a defense of the war in Iran — “bonkers” after Tuesday’s resounding defeat of MAGA, but said it likely resonated with only Trump, which is all that matters.

“He is sending the signal that he’s a tough guy and going to keep fighting,” the former senior defense official says, joking that it sounds like Hegseth used AI to write the speech. “Hey ChatGPT, what are the things that I can say that are most related to the history of MAGA,” the former senior defense official jokes. Related Content Russell Brand Sentenced to More Than 10 Years in Prison After Rape Conviction Houston Mayor Limits Cooperation With ICE: ‘Elections Matter’ Macklemore Says Nine U.S. Venues Turned Down ‘Free Palestine’ Tour: ‘They Got Me F-cked Up’ Trump’s First Big Post-Midterms Move Is Promoting Stephen Miller

But the move is straight from the Roy Cohn playbook. The lawyer who sat at Joe McCarthy’s elbow during the Red Scare and later taught a young Trump how to fight. Admit nothing. Deny everything. Never apologize. And no matter what, claim victory.

It isn’t surprising that the video is tone deaf. Hegseth’s echo chamber of online trolls and yes-men insulates him from the professional officer class that nicknamed him “No Tab Pete” after he failed to earn his Ranger qualification as an infantry officer. One former senior officer tells me Hegseth would never rate high enough to serve in the military’s most elite formations, despite his fascination with special operations.

As the swirl around Hegseth increases, sources say there’s a sense of anticipation. “Folks across the force are listening and waiting,” a senior officer tells me.

While there is optimism that Hegseth might finally be gone, some of my sources — current and former officers and defense officials — didn’t want to get their hopes up after two years of hearing about his pending removal.

“He has an audience or one, and the president stands behind him,” the former senior defense official says. “If you were asking me to put $100 bucks on it, I’d say he’s staying.”

But if Hegseth leaves, some will celebrate because being forced to follow a Fox News weekend host into war strained even the most professional soldiers. Hegseth’s two years at the Pentagon divided the ranks. At first, savvy generals and leaders — particularly in the Army — used the political cover to enact real reforms around procurement and preparing the force for a modern battlefield. But when Hegseth set his sights on them, the very embodiment of the warrior ethos Hegseth worships — generals like Christopher Donahue, who served with America’s most elite units and led the transformation of the Army based on lessons learned in Ukraine — were chopped, seemingly for political reasons. The senior officer says well-qualified officers — both male and female — are taking their names off promotion lists because they don’t want to work closely with the current leadership.

“Those removed were warfighters,” the senior officer says. “More officers than I can count said that it is so much harder to be good at our jobs [under Hegseth].”   Trending Stories Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ U2 Sound Revitalized on ‘Carnaval de Luz’ Del Casher, Guitarist Who Invented the Wah-Wah Pedal, Dead at 88 Russell Brand Sentenced to More Than 10 Years in Prison After Rape Conviction

The senior officer hopes the next defense secretary will bring more professionalism to the Pentagon, with less emphasis on clicks and likes on social media. Most of the people I talked with laughed when Ron DeSantis’ name was floated as a replacement last week. Many expect the former Florida governor to run for the White House in 2028, and tying his reputation to a lame-duck Trump administration would be political suicide.

Most senior leaders tied to this administration watched their political futures disintegrate on Tuesday. Leaving Hegseth to rant from the Pentagon steps as the reckoning comes. Or as Retired Maj. Richard Ojeda, the newly elected democratic congressman from North Carolina representing Fort Bragg, put it on X: “Tick tock, No Tab Pete.”

Disney and Adobe are building generative AI tools together

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The partnership will give Disney ‘early access’ to AI tech for its resorts, cruise line, ESPN graphics, and more.﻿

The partnership will give Disney ‘early access’ to AI tech for its resorts, cruise line, ESPN graphics, and more.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The Walt Disney Company has teamed up with Adobe to develop proprietary “artist-led” generative AI models to support Disney’s production pipeline. The multi-year partnership was announced today at Adobe’s MAX conference, and will see Adobe providing Disney with early access to its latest technologies for everything from Disney’s theme parks and cruise line to ESPN content. Disney and Adobe will also collaborate “to explore emerging technologies, including responsibly developed AI, according to Adobe’s press release:

“With Adobe Firefly Foundry, Disney will work directly with Adobe on generative AI models tailored to Disney’s properties that are human-centered and artist-led. Customized on Disney properties, these proprietary models are trained on unique world knowledge (character attributes, environments, visual styles) to generate commercially safe and production-ready outputs, while accelerating work and enabling the development of more creative projects.”

The announcement builds on the existing relationship between the two companies, with Adobe noting that Disney’s Imagineering R&D division is already using its Firefly AI platform for theme park development. Adobe will contribute to “expand internal creative workflows” at Walt Disney Studios, and identify how AI can be used to shape ESPN content and “enhance the engagement with sports fans.”

This is the latest example of Disney embracing generative AI tools, following a previous deal with OpenAI and involvement with Character.AI’s bankruptcy. Bloomberg reported last week that Disney is gearing up to lay off thousands of staffers and targeting 20 percent annual cost savings, in what’s expected to be its fourth round of job cuts in 2026.

Toronto couple claim landlord is evicting them for “farting too much”

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A Canadian couple and comedy duo are fighting to keep their home after claiming their landlord is trying to evict them over their excessive farting.

S.E. Grummett and Sam Kruger, who perform together as Creepy Boys, have found themselves in a bizarre dispute involving an air-quality monitor at their apartment in Toronto.

The pair claim their landlord believes they’re smoking indoors, causing unusual readings on the device. However, they insist the actual explanation could be something much more embarrassing. Now, they’re raising money to fight their eviction.

In a GoFundMe campaign, the couple explained that the trouble began in December 2025, when their landlord, who lives upstairs, noticed elevated levels of potentially harmful substances in the air.

The two apartments share ventilation, and their landlord was concerned about the readings because of a recently diagnosed lung condition.

Grummett and Kruger said they tried everything from avoiding cooking to showering elsewhere, but the readings continued to rise.

After researching the issue, they discovered that the monitor could be reacting to normal bodily functions, including breathing and, as it turns out, flatulence.

“We are being wrongfully evicted because we fart too much,” they wrote on their fundraiser. “Really. This is not a joke. We wish we were joking.”

However, their landlord reportedly continued to suspect indoor smoking, which the couple denies. They were eventually served an eviction notice stating that the landlord wanted to reclaim their apartment for personal use.

“We believe this to be in bad faith,” they added. “In Ontario, such evictions have to be heard by the Landlord Tenant Board. So, we are going to court.”

Woman panics after finding rats in new apartment and viewers can’t believe it

Landlord sparks debate after tenants trash apartment in response to eviction

The dispute has since reached Ontario’s Landlord and Tenant Board, where a decision remains pending, according to IBTimes.

Meanwhile, the couple’s fundraiser has surpassed its initial CAD $2,800 target, raising more than CAD $3,400 towards legal expenses.

Paul Anka, Stevie Wonder, Cher & More Kennedy Center Honorees Who Were Teenagers When They First Hit the Billboard Hot 100 (Full List)

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Most of the artists on this list weren't teen idols, as such. They simply knew what they wanted to do from an early age and got off to a fast start.

Paul Anka was just 15 – two weeks away from turning 16 – when he first made Billboard’s pop chart in July 1957 with the smash “Diana.” (This was more than a year before Billboard introduced the Hot 100.) Last week, Anka was announced as a 2026 recipient of the Kennedy Center Honors, one of the most prestigious career cappers in the entertainment field.

Anka is the 11th artist who later received the Kennedy Center Honors who first hit the Hot 100 as a teenager. Anka was a bona-fide teen idol when he started out, with such teen-targeted hits as “Put Your Head on My Shoulder” and “Puppy Love,” but most of the artists on this list weren’t teen idols, as such. They simply knew what they wanted to do from an early age and got off to a fast start.

In addition to these 11 artists who received Kennedy Center Honors as individuals, two artists who received the Honors as part of a group first made the Hot 100 as teenagers. Pete Townshend was 19 when The Who first made the Hot 100 with “I Can’t Explain” in March 1965. The Who received the Kennedy Center Honors in 2008. Verdine White was 19 when Earth, Wind & Fire first made the Hot 100 with “Love Is Life” in July 1971. EWF received the Kennedy Center Honors in 2019.

This year’s other Kennedy Center Honorees are songwriter/producer David Foster, actor/director Andy Garcia, playwright David Mamet, and country star Barbara Mandrell. The awards will be presented on Dec. 7 at Capitol One Arena in Washington, D.C.

Here’s a complete list of artists who went on to receive the Kennedy Center Honors who first hit the Hot 100 as teenagers. They are listed in chronological order by the year they received the Kennedy Center Honors.

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 1994

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 1999

First pre-Hot 100 hit: “Hey, Schoolgirl” (Tom & Jerry)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2002

First Hot 100 hit: “Bad Girl” (The Miracles)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2006

First Hot 100 hit: “Your Heart Belongs to Me” (The Supremes)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2007

First Hot 100 hit: “Surfin’” (The Beach Boys)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2007

First Hot 100 hit: “Why? Am I Treated So Bad” (The Staple Singers)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2016

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2017

First Hot 100 hit: “All I Really Want to Do”

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2018

Notes:  Cher’s recording of the Bob Dylan song hit the Hot 100 one week before Sonny & Cher notched their first hit, “I Got You Babe.”

First Hot 100 hit: “Every Beat of My Heart” (Pips)

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2022

Year Received Kennedy Center Honors: 2026

Nintendo Officially Teases New Yoshi Lego Set

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Nintendo teased a new Lego set on Monday, and it will star everyone's favorite shoe-wearing dinosaur. On the Nintendo Today app, Nintendo used a close-up image of Lego Yoshi's face to announce the new Yoshi set.

The announcement said "adults welcome," implying this will be an "adult" Lego set. That essentially just means Yoshi will likely be big, made of many pieces, and expensive. For example, the recent Dragon Ball set featuring Shenron is categorized as an 18+ set and includes over 1,700 pieces for $179.99.

For whatever reason, Nintendo does not allow screenshots of the Nintendo Today app. My Nintendo News was able to snag an image of Yoshi, and you can stare into his giant, expressive eyes below.

The announcement post reads, "New Lego Super Mario set hatching soon." The copyright on the image has 2027 for the year, implying this new Yoshi set won't be out until some time next year.

Lego has sets for every fandom, like Star Trek and The Lord of the Rings, and that includes Nintendo properties. The two companies started collaborating years ago on Mario sets and have continued since. The Mario and Yoshi pixel art set is currently sold out at the Lego store, but the Mario Kart sets are still readily available. On the way next year, in addition to the newly announced Yoshi, are what fans have been waiting for: Super Mario minifigures.

Zelda has also gotten new Lego sets in 2026. The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time Final Battle Lego set was revealed earlier this year, and a set for Link and Epona (the hero's trusty steed), will be available next spring.

Russell Brand Victim Says When She Told Channel 4 Managers About Assault ‘It Was Brushed Under the Carpet’

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

One of Russell Brand’s victims says when she told Channel 4 she had been assaulted by the comedian during a shoot for the network, senior management “brushed [it] under the carpet.”

Brand was sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison on Monday after being convicted of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault last week.

During the sentencing hearing the victims submitted statements about how the assaults had impacted their lives. Related Stories NFL to Face New Trial in Sunday Ticket Class-Action Lawsuit to Determine Financial Damages

One victim, who in 2009 was ordered to give Brand a massage before the pushed his face into her groin and verbally abused her during a shoot, said she had reported the assault to Channel 4 but nothing was done.

“Immediately after the incident I spoke up, and sadly it was brushed under the carpet by senior management,” she said in her statement, which was read out by a lawyer for the Crown Prosecution Service. “This really affected me and had something similar happened again at that time, I know I would not have had the confidence to speak up again. There was a culture at the time which meant I felt I had to just carry on and not talk about it anymore.”

The CEO of Channel 4 at the time was Andy Duncan, who stepped down in Nov. 2009. In 2023 the network’s documentary strand “Dispatches” broadcast the result of a joint investigation with The Sunday Times about Brand in which a number of women alleged they had been sexually assaulted by the comedian. None of the alleged victims were involved in the recent court hearing although one is now believed to be assisting with a further police investigation into other incidents.

She said the assault has continued to impact her life. “Following the incident with Rusell Brand I was really upset. I was wary and on my toes about something similar happening again,” she said in the statement. “I felt I lost my trust in people and felt that my working environment wasn’t safe. From that point onwards I made sure that I wasn’t sitting in a room alone and consciously made sure I had a colleague with me in a professional environment.”

In his sentencing remarks, the judge, Justice Bennathan, referred to the incident, saying it was an example of Brand’s “staggering arrogance” and showed how his “entourage indulged your dreadful behaviour.”

“[The victim] reluctantly pressed her thumbs into your shoulders, then exclaimed how bony you were, leading your acolytes to laugh (possibly at you rather than her), and you to leap up and start insulting her with childish but sexualised comments,” the judge told Brand before handing down the sentence.

The two members of Brand’s entourage accused of ordering the victim to give the massage were Jack Bayles and Gareth Roy. Bayles, who is head of comedy at “I May Destroy You” producer Various Artists Limited, announced following last week’s verdict he would be stepping back from his role until further notice.

In a statement, he said he couldn’t remember the incident but added: “I would never willingly or proactively wish to make anyone feel uncomfortable, unsafe or unsupported at work. If I was ever present when Brand acted inappropriately and I failed to spot it and step in, then I sincerely apologise.”

Variety has contacted Channel 4 for comment.

Street Fighter Officially Removed From Over 1,000 Theaters After Box Office Drop

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Street Fighter is losing its fighting spirit on the big screen.

Street Fighter is one of the most popular video game franchises of all time, particularly when the focus turns to action releases. There have been multiple Street Fighter adaptations over the years, including both live-action and animated movies and TV shows. In terms of live-action films, the franchise first came to theaters through the release of 1994's Street Fighter, which featured names like Jean-Claude Van Damme as Guile and Ming-Na Wen as Chun-Li. The franchise tried to focus on the latter character through a solo film in 2009's Street Fighter: The Legend of Chun-Li, before 2026 brought forth a new take on the franchise.

Now, the new Street Fighter movie is dealing with the loss of 1065 theaters on its fourth domestic weekend (via Box Office Mojo) alongside a box office drop. 2026's Street Fighter started its domestic wide release in a whopping 3575 theaters on October 16. While it is natural for a movie to lose theaters throughout its run, the film had already been removed from 750 theaters by its second weekend, likely as a result of Street Fighter's $30.7 million domestic opening weekend, which came in lower than expected for a video game adaptation that reportedly had a budget of $82 million.

Based on that budget, Street Fighter needs to finish its box office run between $164 million and $205 million to break even, which is becoming hard with a loss of another 1065 theaters and a global box office total of $99.1 million at the time of writing. Street Fighter's new massive loss of theaters coincides with a brutal box office drop for the video game adaptation. The action film brought in just $2.1 million in its fourth and latest domestic opening weekend. That pushed all the way down to outside the Top 10 Movies at the domestic box office for November 6-8.

Street Fighter ended up at #11 on the chart, with several new theatrical arrivals helping push it out of the coveted domestic top 10. One of those happens to be the movie that dominated the list, the live-action sci-fi sequel Godzilla Minus Zero, which broke records with a $31.6 million debut, making it the highest-grossing foreign-language film debut at the domestic box office in history. Fierce competition and the fact that Street Fighter's reviews have not been kind to the movie, as the action-packed release holds a low 42% critics' score based on 146 reviews, likely impacted its performance.

In ScreenRant's Street Fighter review, Brandon Zachary gave the movie a 7 out of 10 score, noting how Street Fighter always picks style over substance throughout its 119-minute runtime. Street Fighter features many beloved characters from the video game franchise, with actors like Noah Centineo as Ken, Andrew Koji as Ryu, Callina Liang as Chun-Li, Cody Rhodes as Guile, Jason Momoa as Blanka, and many more. Street Fighter focuses on Chun-Li recruiting Ken and Ryu to take the fight to the evil M. Bison, played by David Dastmalchian, as they enter a fighting tournament.

Schiff: Saylor's STRC Is 'Dead'

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Earlier today, gold bug Peter Schiff claimed that Strategy executive chairman Michael Saylor was trying to prop up the floundering investment product by carrying out heavy stock buybacks.

"Michael, STRC is dead. You have been keeping it alive on artificial life support with your buybacks," he wrote.

According to Schiff, Strategy is running out of money to prop up its preferred stock. The ongoing Bitcoin market crash is going to aggravate the situation even further, he argued.

"But you're running out of cash. And the selling pressure on STRC will increase as Bitcoin crashes," Schiff wrote.

The Bitcoin bear went on to criticize the generous 12% annualized dividend yield of STRC, saying that ultimately the yield wouldn't be enough to cover the risks of the product.

"It won't be nearly enough yield to cover the risk. 12%," he warned.

The stern comments from Schiff followed a typical bullish tweet from Saylor. In his post, Saylor claimed that AI could not mint new Bitcoin.

"AI can make me rap. It can't print Bitcoin," the cofounder of Strategy wrote.

Strategy's Variable Rate Series A Perpetual Stretch Preferred Stock (STRC) seeks to provide regular dividend income to investors while trading near its $100 face value.

Currently, STRC offers a 12% annual dividend yield, which Strategy has been working to keep steady in order to keep the stock trading near its face value.

However, maintaining the price stability of STRC has come at a big cost.

In its Q3 financial results on Oct. 29, Strategy reported that the firm had spent a total of $2.04 billion on repurchases of STRC as of Oct. 25.

Describing the heavy buybacks as a success, the firm said it had successfully put STRC on the road to financial recovery.

However, these buybacks have come under fire from Schiff, who questions whether the firm can keep propping up investor demand by repurchasing its shares forever.

In spite of the bearish comments from Schiff, Strategy isn't budging on its strategy of ramping up its Bitcoin holdings.

The firm's recent regulatory filing shows that Strategy bought another 1,036 BTC between Nov. 2 and Nov. 8.

The total cost of these purchases is estimated to be $81.9 million, with an average purchase price of $79,012 per BTC.

With the latest purchase, Strategy now holds an eye-watering 850,364 BTC.

Is Krispy Kreme a Buy After Its Latest Earnings Report?

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The iconic doughnut maker just posted an earnings surprise.

After what had been a rough year for the longtime donut maker, Krispy Kreme (DNUT -4.44%), a positive earnings surprise on Nov. 5 sent the stock soaring.

Krispy Kreme reported an $0.08 loss per diluted share in its third quarter, a 3-cent improvement from the same quarter in 2025. Net revenue of nearly $329 million fell 12.5% year over year, largely as expected, due to the company's refranchising efforts.

When adjusting for restructuring expenses, tax-specific adjustments, and other items not core to the operating business, Krispy Kreme reported adjusted earnings per share of $0.06, up from $0.01 one year ago.

Is the stock a buy after Krispy Kreme's third-quarter earnings report?

Krispy Kreme has struggled immensely over the past five years, with its stock down over 72% due to a range of issues, including high debt, competition, and shifting consumer preferences.

The company has also failed to pivot accordingly, with strategic moves such as its partnerships with McDonald's and Walmart proving flawed. Currently, the company is focused on a less capital-intensive strategy that involves refranchising, which CEO Joshua Charlesworth believes can improve returns on capital, expand margins, and drive sustainable growth.

This year, Krispy Kreme has opened 98 doughnut shops globally, nearly all of which are franchised, and decreased its capital expenditures by nearly 65%.

Free cash flow is improving, with the company generating negative free cash flow of nearly $10 million in the quarter, compared with negative $92 million in the third quarter of 2025.

Krispy Kreme has also lowered its net leverage ratio, which is net debt divided by adjusted EBITDA, from 6.7x to 5.5x, largely through net debt reduction and some increase in adjusted EBITDA.

"Today, franchisees generate approximately 42% of our system wide sales and we expect that percentage to grow as we continue to pursue additional refranchising opportunities," Charlesworth said on the company's third-quarter earnings call. "We are disciplined in selecting the right partners both internationally and in the U.S. to maximize value and position the brand for long-term growth."

Krispy Kreme also raised its full-year guidance by increasing and narrowing its net revenue outlook to $1.35 billion to $1.4 billion, up from $1.25 billion to $1.35 billion. The company also lowered its capex outlook to $40 million to $45 million, down from $50 million to $60 million.

Following the earnings report, Wall Street analysts raised their price targets on the stock. Bank of America analyst Sara Senatore maintained a buy rating on the stock and raised her price target from $5.50 to $6.30 per share, implying significant upside from current levels.

The quarter definitely represents good news, as the refranchising efforts appear to be the financially prudent direction moving forward. If Krispy Kreme can keep executing and deleveraging the balance sheet, then there is definitely more upside ahead.

Still, it's likely a bit premature to take on too heavy a position in the doughnut maker just yet, as the impacts of broader changes on the doughnut industry, such as GLP-1 drugs, are still not immediately clear.

The company's high leverage remains a risk if the turnaround doesn't work. I would place Krispy Kreme in the more speculative part of your portfolio for now and watch for continued progress.

Bank of America is an advertising partner of Motley Fool Money. Bram Berkowitz has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Walmart. The Motley Fool recommends the following options: long January 2028 $320 calls on McDonald's and short January 2028 $340 calls on McDonald's. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Why Figs Stock Popped Today

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Investors sold Figs stock after earnings last week, but are having a rethink this morning.

Figs (FIGS +8.12%) stock, a supplier of scrubs to the medical industry, dropped 2% on Friday despite beating earnings in its Q3 report -- but don't fret. Figs is bouncing back this morning, up 7.8% through 10:20 a.m. ET and, in fact, trading 5% above where it stood before reporting earnings.

Figs grew its sales 26% year over year to $190.7 million in Q3, beating analyst forecasts for $182.8 million. Earnings of $0.10 per share were $0.03 better than the analyst estimate of $0.07 -- and twice the nickel Figs earned in Q3 last year.

Sales in the company's core scrubs business grew a fine 24% year over year, while the company saw faster growth in newer businesses -- non-scrubwear clothing, where sales grew 35%, and international sales, where sales soared 51%.

Figs furthermore held operating cost growth to less than 14%, dramatically expanding profit margins. On the bottom line, Figs netted 10.1% of each $1 sold as profit.

Figs enters Q4 with "sustainable top- and bottom-line momentum as we look ahead to 2027," says CEO Trina Spear, although the company's CFO is forecasting only 15% sales growth in the quarter -- much slower growth than Figs saw in Q3. This may be what worried investors last week, and caused the share price to slip Friday.

Still, the company is looking to close out 2026 with 23.5% revenue growth, and earnings growth probably much stronger than that. At a price-to-earnings ratio of 52x, Figs stock isn't exactly cheap, but if it keeps growing like it's going, Figs stock just might still be a buy.

Rich Smith has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Figs. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

UK considering banning under 16s from eBay to “keep children safe”

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The UK government is considering banning children under 16 from using eBay, Vinted, and other online marketplaces as part of its growing crackdown on platforms that allow young users to interact with strangers.

Britain is already preparing to ban under-16s from major social media platforms, including Instagram, TikTok, and Snapchat, with restrictions expected to take effect in spring 2027.

However, the government could go even further, potentially preventing children from using popular shopping websites over concerns about online grooming.

According to Sky News, Culture Secretary Lisa Nandy confirmed that officials are considering expanding the ban to online marketplaces.

Asked during an appearance on the BBC’s Sunday with Laura Kuenssberg whether eBay and Vinted could be included, Nandy responded: “We are looking seriously at doing so in order to keep children safe.”

The Sunday Times reports that Depop and Facebook Marketplace could also be affected, with officials concerned that messaging features allow adults to contact and potentially groom children.

Research from UK regulator Ofcom found that 19% of children aged eight to 14 use eBay monthly, while 15% use Vinted.

The Children’s Society has also warned that vulnerable children using resale platforms have been targeted by adults offering money in exchange for explicit photographs or attempting to lure them with drugs.

Interestingly, eBay already requires account holders to be at least 18 years old, while Vinted generally has the same minimum age but allows parents or guardians to create supervised accounts for minors.

Canada could restrict Roblox & Minecraft for under 16s as part of social media ban

EU plans sweeping social media and AI chatbot restrictions for kids under 15

UK confirms plans to ban social media for under-16s

The proposals are the latest sign that governments are looking beyond traditional social media when deciding which online platforms children should be allowed to access.

Earlier in 2026, UK officials considered preventing children from talking to strangers in games like Roblox, Fortnite, and Minecraft.

Canada has taken a similar approach, introducing legislation to restrict social media access for under-16s before considering expanding its rules to gaming platforms.

Meanwhile, Australia implemented its own under-16 social media ban in December 2025, targeting services including Instagram, TikTok, YouTube, Twitch, and Kick. The restrictions have already faced legal challenges, with Reddit suing the Australian government over privacy and freedom of expression concerns.

For now, the UK has not confirmed whether online marketplaces will be included in its restrictions, with further details expected as officials finalize which platforms will be affected.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/healthtap-unveils-virtual-primary-care-program-medicare-advantage-plans" hreflang="en">HealthTap unveils virtual primary care program for Medicare Advantage plans</a>

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →
HealthTap unveils virtual primary care program for Medicare Advantage plans" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

HealthTap is rolling out a new program for Medicare Advantage plans that seeks to help members form a connection with a primary care doctor that carries forward.

The program leans on a combination of proactive outreach and access to a patient's historical medical records to support plans in identifying needs or risks, filling care gaps and coordinating follow-up or other necessary care. It's built to directly support individuals who may not have a PCP on file.

HealthTap reaches out to those members proactively, the company said, and helps them schedule an initial virtual visit with a provider who stays connected for preventive services, chronic condition management and medication concerns.

"The angle that we're taking is: wouldn't it be cool if, in fact, your virtual primary care physician was just like a bricks and mortar primary care physician?" said Rick Cochran, senior vice president of health plans at HealthTap, in an interview with Fierce Healthcare.

In an ideal scenario, a patient who has a consistent primary care physician who they see over the course of multiple years and who they come to trust, he said. HealthTap is looking to build that experience with virtual primary care docs, and reach patients who are disconnected from the health system with critical services.

Cochran said that the team, at times, receives feedback about concern that a program like this could upset in-network practices. However, he said that HealthTap views these service lines as complementary, and the company as offering another way for insurers to reach members who are underserved and in need of help.

"There's a bunch of people who haven't been receiving care that we can work with," he said. "We're not attempting to disrupt anything. We're attempting to complement things."

Through the program, HealthTap can connect with members via text messaging, email, direct mail or phone, at the plan's discretion. The member can then schedule the appointment for themselves and choose a board-certified primary care physician based in their state.

Between video visits, the member can also connect with their physician through secure messaging, HealthTap said.

In aiming to make the experience feel as much like traditional, brick-and-mortar clinics as possible, Cochran said that members are allowed to switch their physicians and find the one that best matches their individual health needs.

And to better support the physicians, HealthTap pulls from health information networks to pull key medical records, including relevant data from before an individual enrolled in their current plan. That set-up also means that the patient can carry their relationship with a HealthTap doc forward should they switch plans, Cochran said.

Cochran said that COVID-19 really kicked down the door to virtual care for seniors, and there's significant opportunity in MA for a program like HealthTap's, as these patients often have multiple comorbidities and may face barriers to seeing a doctor in person, such as the lack of transportation.

The company did an initial deployment with one MA plan and found that 35% of the initial outreach cohort scheduled an appointment, and 19% completed their first visit within six weeks. Satisfaction scores for both patients and physicians are high, Cochran said.

The approach gives doctors more time to spend working "to make people better, not crank through visits," he said.

"Our physician satisfaction score is very high because our physicians have the time to spend with the patient to ensure that the patient's needs are met," he said.

Nvidia’s GR00T 2 teaches robots delicate skills from human video

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

The open model learns hand skills partly from human video, and Foxconn and Siemens are already testing it.

Nvidia unveiled GR00T 2, an open AI model that helps robots learn delicate hand skills, trained partly on 100,000 hours of human video. It reached 95% average success with five demonstrations per task in Nvidia’s tests. Foxconn, Siemens and Nexuni are testing it.

Nvidia has unveiled GR00T 2, an AI model that helps robots learn delicate hand skills partly by watching videos of people, the company said on Monday. It announced the model at the Conference on Robot Learning in Austin, Texas.

Nvidia calls GR00T 2 an open “world action model,” one that predicts how its actions will change what it sees before it moves.

It was trained on robot data and human video, including 100,000 hours of first-person footage that Nvidia is releasing for anyone to use. That lets robots learn skills from people and pass them between robots with different bodies, Nvidia says. Startups such as Maxinsights pay people to film their chores for the same purpose.

In Nvidia’s tests, GR00T 2 did 40 of 50 tasks at least once with no training for the task. Shown a task once, it succeeded 71% of the time on average, and 95% when shown five times. Nvidia’s own paper notes those tasks were picked from ones the model could already learn well.

Foxconn, which now earns most of its revenue from AI servers, is using GR00T 2 to help build them. Siemens is testing it for factory work and Nexuni is trying it for laundry. None has said it will use it in production yet.

Nvidia also released Isaac Lab 3.0, its open-source tool for training robots in simulation. It says the tool trains up to 2.5 times faster on one test. Partners include Walden Robotics, which launched with $300M in July.

“To me, agentic robotics is the most exciting technological development since the internet in 1995,” said Ken Goldberg, a robotics professor at UC Berkeley who worked with Nvidia on two of the projects.

Earlier GR00T models already underpin robots in development, including LG’s humanoid due in 2027. Siemens has also tested an Nvidia-powered humanoid on an eight-hour factory shift.

BYD is going all out on its ultra-luxury EV with coach doors and 1,000+ km of range [Images]

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

BYD unveiled the Yangwang Altior, its new ultra-luxury EV with coach doors, over 1,000 km of CTLC range, a 1,000V fast-charging platform, and plenty of premium extras.

The “super-luxury executive flagship sedan,” as BYD calls it, made its official debut in Abu Dhabi, UAE, over the weekend.

For those who don’t know, Yangwang is BYD’s ultra-luxury sub-brand, sitting at the very top of its brand hierarchy.

The Atrio is Yangwang’s largest and most luxurious sedan, set to compete with high-end brands like Rolls-Royce, Bentley, and Mercedes-Benz Maybach.

Measuring 5,600 mm long, 2,030 mm wide, and 1,590 mm tall, with a 3,500 mm wheelbase, BYD’s flagship sedan sits squarely between the Rolls-Royce Ghost and Phantom (standard wheelbase) in length.

BYD said the “Golden Body Proportions” are designed to maximize interior space for both front and rear passengers.

Its meticulous front-end design is inspired by “Ding,” a traditional ancient Chinese cauldron, featuring “diamond-cut” headlights, a 137-gemstone dot-matrix grille, and, to top it off, a 24K pure gold car emblem sits up front and center.

The coach doors create a grand entrance, with the front doors opening at an 80-degree angle and the rear doors opening to a 90-degree angle.

Inside, the flagship sedan takes luxury up a notch with a massive, full-length panoramic center infotainment screen that extends from the driver to the passenger side.

The “Wrap-around cockpit” is designed to create an immersive, premium experience, with four executive seats and a central island for rear passengers that includes vehicle controls, storage, and even a champagne glass holder.

BYD said it offers the most second-row space in its class, but didn’t reveal specifics. The zero-gravity “cloud-like” rear seats feature a four-ball mechanical massage head, you know, for those longer trips.

Meanwhile, the front passenger seat can fold at the push of a button to open up more space for rear passengers.

The interior features premium materials, including full-grain genuine leather seats, diamond-cut detailing, real wood trim, and natural lambswool foot pads.

And that’s not all. It also has a 39-speaker Dynaudio Platinum Evidence high-end sound system that creates an immersive 360-degree “hall-level” sound experience.

The Yangwang Altio is packed with BYD’s latest breakthrough tech, including a high-speed tire-blowout stability system, emergency float mode, and in-place U-turn or tank-turn control using its quad-motor e4 platform.

It also uses BYD’s second-generation Blade Batteries and Fast Charging Technology, which provides a CLTC driving range of over 1,000 km (621 miles).

The flagship sedan features “extremely fast flash charging,” BYD said, thanks to its 1000V ultra flash charging tech. It takes just five minutes to charge the vehicle’s battery from 10% to 70%. To go from 10% to 97% it takes just nine minutes.

BYD opened orders for the limited-edition “Mountain and River Rhyme” Yangwang Altrio model, which will be extremely exclusive with just 399 units.

Buyers can place a reservation with a 300,000 yuan ($45,000) deposit. BYD didn’t reveal final prices, but the global version is expected to start at around 2 million yuan ($300,000).

Meta expands Instagram’s AI age-guessing tech globally

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Meta announced on Monday that it’s expanding its AI-powered age estimation technology on Instagram worldwide to identify more teens and place them under its Teen Account protections, as it faces numerous lawsuits accusing its platforms of harming teens.

The social media giant says it will use the AI technology to find people it suspects to be teens, even if they use an adult birthday.

Teen Accounts place users into a stricter account experience with additional safeguards, such as receiving direct messages only from people they follow or are already connected to, hiding harmful comments, and setting accounts to private by default.

Meta’s age-guessing tools use AI to analyze entire profiles for contextual clues, such as birthday celebrations or mentions of school grades. Meta also looks for these signals across different formats, such as posts, comments, bios, captions, and more. The company also looks at visual cues from photos and videos, including a person’s height or bone structure.

Meta said it’s continuing to roll out the technology on Facebook, with a similar global rollout planned for Threads later this year.

The global expansion comes as Meta reached settlements worth up to $18 billion with nearly every U.S. state and territory in August to resolve lawsuits alleging its platforms harm teens. As part of the settlements, Meta agreed to improve how it identifies teens and children under 13.

“We’ll continue to do our part to proactively ensure teens are in age-appropriate experiences on our apps,” Meta wrote in a blog post. “That’s why as part of our agreement with 52 bipartisan attorneys general across US states, territories, and the District of Columbia, we’re investing in even stronger technology to proactively catch accounts that may belong to children under 13. We’re also strengthening the technology we use to identify accounts that may belong to teens between the ages of 13 and 17 so we can ensure those accounts are placed in experiences designed for teens, even if they give us an adult birthday.”

Meta said parents in the U.S. will start seeing an in-feed video on Instagram explaining its approach to ensuring their teens are in age-appropriate protections.

Ripple Takes the Upper Hand Against Ethereum's Ethena in Stablecoin Race

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. Ripple USD (RLUSD) now tops the chart for short-term volume growth on decentralized exchanges (DEXs), outpacing Ethena's USDe. RWA Foundation, citing Token Terminal data, shows RLUSD's DEX trading volume jumped by $127.4 million over the past seven days.

USDe, the synthetic dollar issued by Ethena, has a bigger market cap of $4.97 billion at press time. Its DEX volume grew by $71.4 million, giving RLUSD a $56 million lead for the week.

Other major DeFi stablecoins, Usual's USD0 and Aave's GHO among them, stayed below $20 million.

Ethena offers roughly 5% yield through sUSDe staking. According to Ethena's documentation, the main risk of its delta-neutral model, which relies on short positions in perpetual futures, is prolonged negative funding rates.

USDe supply peaked above $14 billion in 2025. Ethena's fee switch and ENA buybacks are set to start only once the 14-day average USDe supply reaches $7.5 billion.

RLUSD is issued under the supervision of the New York State Department of Financial Services (NYDFS) and backed 100% by cash and short-term U.S. Treasuries, with monthly attestations of its reserves.

BNY acts as the custodian of RLUSD reserves.

In late October, the US Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) gave final approval to Ripple National Trust Bank. The bank will manage RLUSD reserves under federal oversight.

Besides it, RLUSD is available in Spark's Stablecoin FX pool on Uniswap v4, which lets corporate players make multi-million-dollar swaps.

According to RWA Foundation, about 92% of RLUSD turnover on the XRP Ledger (XRPL) runs through direct pairs with XRP.

Ripple also expanded its partnership with Robinhood. According to the companies, Bitstamp will use RLUSD on the XRP Ledger for settlement, while Robinhood plans to offer RLUSD to its retail customers by the end of the year.

At the same time, Coinbase added RLUSD to Coinbase Prime, Coinbase Exchange and its retail app, with 1:1 USD conversion on the XRP Ledger, Ethereum and Base.

At press time, RLUSD's market cap stands at $2.69 billion, roughly half USDe's size. The stablecoin started 2026 at about $1.28 billion.

Elizabeth Warren Plans Prediction Market Crackdown as She Prepares to Chair Senate Banking

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Sen. Elizabeth Warren plans to crack down on prediction markets once she takes the Senate Banking Committee gavel in January, with a banking aide telling Punchbowl News that Warren would seek to stop the sector’s “Wild West-style lack of oversight.”

The Massachusetts Democrat is poised to chair the panel after Democrats swept both chambers of Congress last week, putting her in charge of a portfolio that covers banking, housing, the Federal Reserve and cryptocurrency. Punchbowl published the aide’s rundown of her agenda on Monday.

Warren intends to use the committee’s investigative powers for oversight of prediction markets and election betting platforms, aimed at insider trading and platform manipulation — the same fight she opened before the midterms.

That earlier push was bipartisan. On Oct. 28, Warren and Sen. John Curtis, R-Utah, wrote to Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour and Polymarket CEO Shayne Coplan asking how the companies stop candidates, campaign staff and others with material nonpublic information from trading election contracts.

“We are particularly troubled by election-related event contracts,” the senators wrote. “Event contracts tied to elections receive prominent coverage and are treated by some observers as indicators of political momentum. There are also documented cases of candidates betting on their own races, which undermines the overall integrity of the American electoral process.”

The letter cited candidates who traded on their own contests on Kalshi, including a February 2026 California gubernatorial race, and pointed to research alleging wash trading and volume manipulation on Polymarket. It also referenced an NPR investigation of campaign insiders who said they traded ahead of polling releases.

“The identification of isolated instances of improper trading does not demonstrate that these markets are adequately protected,” Warren and Curtis wrote. “Instead, it raises serious questions about how much similar activity may be going undetected.” They asked for written answers by Election Day, Nov. 3.

Kalshi’s head of enforcement, Robert DeNault, told Business Insider that the company “proactively blocks candidates and campaign staff from trading to protect market integrity.” Polymarket did not respond to that request for comment.

Prediction markets are not a side show for bitcoin readers. Polymarket is a crypto-native venue that settled U.S. access through a CFTC-registered exchange after years offshore, while Kalshi operates as a designated contract market under the Commodity Futures Trading Commission. Both sit in the orbit of the same committee Warren is about to run.

Under Chairman Michael S. Selig, the CFTC has spent 2026 defending those platforms against state gambling laws, suing Wisconsin, New York and others to assert exclusive federal jurisdiction over event contracts on registered exchanges. A Democratic Banking chair targeting what her aide called a Wild West lands as the counterweight to that stance.

Warren has been one of crypto’s sharpest critics on the Hill. She opposed the CLARITY Act market structure bill, which failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15, and analysts have argued a Democratic Congress leaves little path for a Republican rewrite. House Democrats are already sketching their own package with a bigger SEC role.

The aide’s broader agenda for Warren also includes affordability legislation, a push to cap credit card interest rates, AI chip export controls, and oversight of Trump administration officials, Wall Street CEOs, Big Tech executives and anyone tied to the Trump family’s crypto ventures.

The test for prediction markets is no longer whether Trump-era regulators will keep clearing the way. It is whether Warren, with subpoena power and the Banking gavel, turns the Kalshi and Polymarket letter into hearings that rewrite how election and event contracts are policed.

Plant-based dairy swaps may leave a key nutrient behind

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

A UK modeling study suggests replacing dairy with plant-based alternatives could deepen iodine shortfalls, especially among teenage girls and women.

Plant-based milk, yogurt and cheese make it easy to cut back on dairy without rethinking every meal.

In the UK, though, dairy is one of the main places people get iodine, a mineral the body uses to make thyroid hormones. Most plant-based versions contain very little of it.

A new modeling study estimates that swapping about a third of dairy for store-bought plant-based products would leave more teenage girls and young women short of iodine.

The same model also tested a fix, and adding iodine to both the plant-based products and bread kept every age group at least as well supplied as before.

Researchers at the University of Surrey and Queen’s University Belfast asked no one to change their diet.

The study, led by Katie Nicol, used real food diaries and calculated how much of four vitamins and minerals people would get if some of their dairy were replaced.

“With the rise in plant-based alternative foods, we need to know whether they are like-for-like replacements for the micronutrients that are found in dairy products,” said Nicol.

Cow’s milk in the UK is rich in iodine partly because farmers add it to cattle feed to keep animals healthy. Iodine-containing disinfectants used in dairy production add to it.

Many countries add iodine to table salt. The UK doesn’t have that policy, and iodized salt is hard to find in British shops, so dairy has become the country’s main iodine source.

In the team’s earlier survey of UK supermarkets, only 12% of plant-based alternatives had added iodine, and even those often contained less than cow’s milk.

Sarah Bath, an associate professor in public health nutrition at the University of Surrey, was also involved in the study.

“People may also be unaware of the importance of iodine, which is needed to make thyroid hormones to regulate our metabolism, support healthy growth and brain development,” said Bath.

The team started with the UK’s National Diet and Nutrition Survey.

Between 2016 and 2019, the survey asked 3,558 people, from 18-month-olds to adults up to 100, to write down everything they ate for four days.

The researchers focused on the 3,095 people who ate some dairy during those days, since there’s nothing to swap in a diet without it.

On an average day, these people had about 237 grams of milk, cheese and yogurt.

The team then replaced that dairy, gram for gram, with plant-based versions from UK supermarket websites. Each one was picked at random, so the results reflected how widely those products vary.

The researchers tested four levels of change, replacing 20%, 35%, 50% or all of a person’s dairy. The first two match the UK climate plan’s call to cut meat and dairy by 20% by 2030 and 35% by 2050.

The 50% level reflects international advice to cut animal foods worldwide by more than half. Dairy contributes substantially to greenhouse gas emissions.

Teenage girls were the worst off from the start, and those aged 14 to 18 ate less dairy than any other group.

The team estimated that 54% of them fell short of the daily iodine benchmark nutritionists use to judge whether a group is getting enough.

In a population with enough iodine, only about 2% to 3% of people would fall below that line.

The model estimates that replacing just over a third of their dairy would push the share of teenage girls falling short to 65%.

For women of childbearing age, 19 to 49, it would climb from 32% to 42%.

The authors wrote that iodine is especially important during pregnancy, infancy and adolescence.

If all dairy were replaced, the model estimates that 80% of teenage girls and 72% of teenage boys would fall short.

Young children, teenagers and adult women had the biggest drops overall.

Calcium, riboflavin (a B vitamin) and vitamin B12 intakes also fell, but shortfalls became more common only when half or all of the dairy was replaced. Iodine was the most sensitive of the four.

Next, the team tested three fixes. The first required every plant-based product to contain all four nutrients, at the minimum level needed to be labeled a source of each one.

The requirement kept average intakes steady, but it didn’t fix the shortfall teenage girls already had. Between 56% and 59% of them still fell below the iodine benchmark.

The second option was bread, which six in 10 people in the survey ate. Australia and New Zealand already require bakers to use iodized salt in bread.

In the model, doing the same with all commercially made bread cut the share of teenage girls short on iodine from 54% to 37%.

For women aged 19 to 49, iodized bread helped less as dairy disappeared.

Their shortfall dropped from 32% to 24% when a fifth of their dairy was replaced, but rose to 44% once all of it was gone.

The best results came from doing both. With iodine added to plant-based products and to bread, every age group kept or improved its iodine supply, even with no dairy at all.

None of the policies raised the share of any group getting too much iodine.

“Nutrition policy must evolve in tandem with environmental targets; however, it is important that shifts towards more sustainable diets do not compromise on public health,” Nicol said.

The study is a set of calculations, not a trial in which people changed what they ate. It assumes everyone shifts in the same way, even though interest in plant-based products varies with age, sex and culture.

Without sales figures, the model gave every product the same chance of being picked, though real shoppers don’t buy them equally.

The product data also come from a 2020 survey, and recipes in this market may change.

Nor did the team look at how well the body absorbs nutrients from fortified foods. People who ate no dairy at all, 13% of the survey, were left out, so it’s unknown how fortification would affect them.

Whether real diets will shift the way the model assumes remains an open question.

The authors called for continued tracking of what people eat, and how much of each nutrient they get, as the food supply keeps changing.

The full study was published in the journal The Lancet Planetary Health.

Hoarseness but no bounce: New manager Scott Parker has his work cut out

Steven Spielberg’s 140-Minute Sci-Fi Blockbuster Officially Surges on Streaming

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Lade Omotade is a News and Features Author at Collider, covering everything from casting announcements and streaming trends to behind-the-scenes stories and the cultural impact of today's biggest film and TV franchises. Since joining the team in 2024, she has written more than 1,000 news stories, with her work also cited by outlets like AOL and IMDb.

Steven Spielberg rarely disappoints with his science fiction films, many of which have earned him high praise over the years, most notably his classics E.T. the Extra-Terrestrial and Jurassic Park. Even his most recent work, Disclosure Day, starring Emily Blunt, which divided critics and viewers upon release, still found its way to the top of multiple streaming and digital charts, earning strong redemption in no time.

About eleven years ago, Spielberg signed on to direct one of his most ambitious sci-fi blockbusters, which officially hit theaters in March 2018. The film was a critical and commercial success, amassing a huge following that has grown over the years. It currently holds a Certified Fresh 71% rating on Rotten Tomatoes, based on 443 reviews, and grossed $608 million worldwide against a $155–175 million budget at the time of its release.

The action film’s success has unsurprisingly led to a sequel, which doesn’t yet have a release date. Meanwhile, as fans await further updates about a new film, its predecessor is climbing the streaming charts. Ready Player One is back in the streaming spotlight, ranking among the Top 10 movies on Apple TV internationally. Over the past few days, it has ranked as the tenth most-watched movie, just behind Anya Taylor-Joy’s Apple TV sci-fi original, The Gorge, often shifting to 11th place.

Directed by Spielberg and written by Zak Penn and Ernest Cline, Ready Player One is based on Cline's bestselling 2011 novel. Cline, who also wrote the 2020 book sequel Ready Player Two, confirmed in December of that year that a film sequel was in the early stages of development. Spielberg later confirmed in March 2024 that the new film was still in development, adding that, unlike its predecessor, he would produce but not direct it. Now, screenwriter Penn has shared the latest update, confirming in August 2026 that the sequel is still in the works.

Starring Tye Sheridan and Olivia Cooke, Ready Player One is set in 2045, when humanity uses the OASIS, a virtual reality simulation, to escape the real world. Teenage orphan Wade Watts (Sheridan) discovers clues to a contest that promises the winner ownership of the OASIS. He and his friends try to complete it before an evil corporation can take control of it themselves. The film also stars Ben Mendelsohn, Lena Waithe, T.J. Miller, Simon Pegg, and Mark Rylance.

Ready Player One streams on Apple TV in international territories.

Red Hat adds agentic AI operations, stronger security in OpenShift 5 and RHEL updates

📅 2026-11-09  |  Read Source →

Red Hat Inc. today announced a major update to its OpenShift Kubernetes-based application development platform that adds artificial intelligence-powered troubleshooting, stronger security controls and simplified upgrades as the company seeks to make its hybrid cloud infrastructure easier to manage.

The company also announced new versions of Red Hat Enterprise Linux with enhanced protection against software tampering, AI-assisted system administration and post-quantum cryptography.

The releases, unveiled at KubeCon + CloudNativeCon North America in Salt Lake City, reflect Red Hat’s continuing initiatives to embed AI into enterprise infrastructure management while strengthening security across increasingly distributed environments.

Red Hat OpenShift 5, the first major version upgrade in seven years, extends the platform’s ability to manage containers, virtual machines and AI workloads within a common environment. It also broadens the OpenShift Lightspeed generative AI assistant into an agent capable of investigating operational problems and recommending corrective actions.

“It’s infusing intelligence into the platform with agent-based troubleshooting through a simple interface with Lightspeed and natural language,” said Chris Wright, Red Hat’s chief technology officer and senior vice president of global engineering.

The updated Lightspeed interface allows administrators to generate visualizations of system performance using natural-language requests. For example, an administrator can ask for central processing unit utilization over the previous week, add memory consumption and overlay application errors. The resulting charts can be saved to dashboards that update continuously.

More significantly, OpenShift 5 introduces agentic troubleshooting that automatically investigates cluster events. When problems occur, the system can examine related information, reason through possible causes and propose remediation steps. Administrators retain control over whether the recommended actions are executed.

The platform also includes an AI-driven upgrade planner that examines application dependencies, Kubernetes application programming interfaces and optional operators to identify potential compatibility problems before updates begin.

“It is easy to upgrade Kubernetes,” said Mike Barrett, Red Hat’s vice president and general manager of hybrid cloud platforms. “What is difficult is navigating all the things you’re running on top of Kubernetes.”

Security enhancements include hardened multitenancy, more extensive network isolation, automated certificate management and post-quantum cryptography for control-plane communications. The capabilities are aimed in part at sovereign cloud providers and regulated organizations that must maintain strict separation between tenants and demonstrate compliance with security requirements.

OpenShift 5 also supports automated graphics processing unit resource allocation across AI inference and traditional enterprise workloads, potentially improving utilization of that expensive computing infrastructure.

Unlike previous major platform transitions, OpenShift 5 can be installed as an in-place update from OpenShift 4, avoiding the need to reinstall software or migrate workloads, Red Hat said. A revised lifecycle approach provides greater predictability for customers operating long-lived enterprise applications and virtualized infrastructure.

“We are no longer asking you to reinstall or migrate to use more of our product,” Barrett said. “This is a simple upgrade.”

The company is emphasizing virtualization as an increasingly important part of its business. Barrett said OpenShift Virtualization has grown to an $800 million business as customers seek alternatives to traditional virtualization platforms.

It said NASA’s laboratory migrated approximately 2,400 virtual machines to OpenShift Virtualization, while Gates Corp. moved more than 1,300 virtual machines and reported a 50% increase in available compute capacity.

The forthcoming Red Hat Enterprise Linux 10.3 and 9.9 releases extend the company’s security strategy to the operating system.

A principal addition in RHEL 10.3 is support for sealed images, which use customer-controlled cryptographic keys to verify operating system integrity from the bootloader through the kernel and root file system. The technology is designed to prevent unauthorized modifications and block compromised software components from running.

Red Hat is also introducing a Model Context Protocol server that allows compatible AI assistants to investigate live Linux systems while maintaining access controls. Integration with Performance Co-Pilot, an open-source toolkit for monitoring, recording, analyzing, and controlling infrastructure, enables agents to examine historical performance information alongside current system conditions to identify the causes of operational problems.

A self-managed version of the RHEL command-line assistant, available in developer preview, lets organizations run AI inference locally. The capability is intended for disconnected, air-gapped and regulated environments where sending operational information outside the organization may be prohibited.

The releases also introduce post-quantum cryptography support for applications built using the Go programming language, including integration of the post-quantum Module-Lattice-Based Key-Encapsulation Mechanism encryption algorithm with the language’s Transport Layer Security implementation. Additional enhancements include updated developer toolchains and support for newer processor architectures.

Red Hat separately announced Developer Hub 2.1, an update to its enterprise developer portal that gives AI coding agents governed access to organizational information, approved software components and standardized development workflows. Through MCP integration, agents can discover existing services, follow established development procedures and perform authorized actions using enterprise tooling.

The update also introduces expanded AI asset cataloging, a context-aware assistant and a longer support lifecycle, increasing the release support window from six to 10 months. Developer Hub 2.1 is generally available, as is OpenShift 5, while the new RHEL versions are expected in the coming weeks.
